> **Evidence rule:** “Implemented foundation” means a concrete repository artifact is linked. It does not mean that representative scale, production sizing or an enterprise performance runtime has been proven. “Partial,” “Scaffold,” “Planned,” “Future,” and “Conceptual target architecture” are deliberately distinct states.

## Document control

This architecture is pending Architecture Board, Performance Engineering, Platform Engineering, Database Engineering, Operations, Security, Integration, Reporting, Finance, Inventory, Manufacturing, Mobile, Data Governance, Internal Audit and Cost Management review. It governs evidence and decisions; it does not authorize code, data, infrastructure, topology, vendor, deployment or production-capacity changes.

## Chapter 01 — Purpose and Scope

This volume governs how FlowCraft states, measures and approves performance before production-scale implementation. Its audience is the Architecture Board, engineering and operations disciplines, domain owners, assurance functions and cost management; it excludes code, topology changes, procurement commitments and unmeasured production sizing.

### Governed performance contract

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |
|---|---|---|---|
| Requirement | governed scope | Define governed scope as a versioned contract with an explicit measurement point. | Performance Architecture |
| Evidence | workload evidence | Supply scale-shaped evidence for workload evidence without substituting an average or anecdote. | Domain owner |
| Protection | non-authorization | Escalate non-authorization when the approved workload exceeds safe capacity or authority is at risk. | Operations |

### Relationships and exclusions

FCSB-001 through FCSB-021 establish business, platform, data, security, deployment and domain authority; [FCSB-022](./FCSB-Volume-22-Mobile-and-Offline-Architecture.md) supplies the offline synchronization contract whose load is modeled here. FCSB-024 and FCSB-025 remain future controlled volumes. This volume does not choose vendors, provision infrastructure, change code, set real production sizes or authorize a release.

## Chapter 02 — Executive Summary

The accepted system is a compact NestJS, Next.js, Prisma and PostgreSQL foundation with useful query constraints but no enterprise performance runtime. The target couples journey budgets to versioned workload models, protects transactional authority from competing work, and admits scale changes only after repeatable capacity evidence.

### Governed performance contract

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |
|---|---|---|---|
| Requirement | runtime summary | Define runtime summary as a versioned contract with an explicit measurement point. | Performance Architecture |
| Evidence | target control loop | Supply scale-shaped evidence for target control loop without substituting an average or anecdote. | Domain owner |
| Protection | maturity distinction | Escalate maturity distinction when the approved workload exceeds safe capacity or authority is at risk. | Operations |

### Maturity conclusion

Current maturity is **Partial foundation**: normal application behaviors can be inspected, but no accepted measurements establish capacity. Target maturity requires versioned workloads, percentile budgets, tenant attribution, test automation, operational telemetry and decision governance before any scale mechanism can be represented as implemented.

## Chapter 03 — Performance and Scalability Principles

Performance is a governed non-functional requirement whose three independent axes are latency, throughput and concurrency. Percentiles replace averages for critical journeys; finite connections, bounded retries, tenant fairness, security, audit and consistency remain hard constraints even when demand exceeds safe capacity.

### Governed performance contract

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |
|---|---|---|---|
| Requirement | percentile discipline | Define percentile discipline as a versioned contract with an explicit measurement point. | Performance Architecture |
| Evidence | finite resources | Supply scale-shaped evidence for finite resources without substituting an average or anecdote. | Domain owner |
| Protection | authority preservation | Escalate authority preservation when the approved workload exceeds safe capacity or authority is at risk. | Operations |

### Mandatory rules

A target without a workload model is invalid. Peak differs from sustained demand; latency, throughput and concurrency are separate; availability objectives name their performance and recovery dependency; read replicas never accept authoritative writes; caches are reconstructible; bulk and sync work are bounded; queues expose business failure; scale-out externalizes state; partitioning and sharding require proof; and optimization never bypasses authorization, scope, audit, reconciliation or integrity.

### Authority boundaries

| Authority | Owned performance responsibility | Boundary that cannot move |
|---|---|---|
| Performance Architecture | Standards, budgets, workload method, capacity evidence and regression governance | Does not operate runtime or redefine business semantics. |
| Operations | Runtime capacity, utilization, scaling execution, incidents and operational telemetry | Does not approve domain consistency shortcuts. |
| Platform Engineering | API mechanisms, concurrency, prospective cache/queue/worker mechanisms | Does not own database tuning or business authority. |
| Database Engineering | Query/index tuning, pools, database capacity and future replica/partition/shard mechanisms | Does not route authoritative writes to stale stores. |
| Domain owners | Transaction semantics, consistency and workload priority | Do not alter shared platform controls unilaterally. |
| Reporting | Certified analytical semantics and scheduling | Does not starve OLTP or certify stale data silently. |
| Security | Authentication, authorization, abuse limits and security telemetry | Controls are not disabled to improve latency. |
| Integration | Partner rates, throttles, retry/backoff and backlog ownership | Retries cannot consume unbounded ERP reserve. |
| Finance | Close, posting and reconciliation workload semantics | Posting authority remains with Finance. |
| Inventory | Stock-transaction workload semantics | Inventory truth is never degraded for speed. |
| Manufacturing | Production-execution workload semantics | Completion and genealogy authority remain intact. |
| Mobile Product Engineering | Client retry, pacing and synchronization behavior | Server receipts remain authoritative under FCSB-022. |

## Chapter 04 — Current Performance Baseline

Repository evidence proves a single Compose API, web and PostgreSQL topology, a Prisma client that connects on module start, a database-backed health response, numerous relational indexes, bounded common pagination and small report previews. It does not prove representative latency, throughput, concurrency, saturation limits or production capacity.

### Governed performance contract

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |
|---|---|---|---|
| Requirement | single topology | Define single topology as a versioned contract with an explicit measurement point. | Performance Architecture |
| Evidence | bounded query foundations | Supply scale-shaped evidence for bounded query foundations without substituting an average or anecdote. | Domain owner |
| Protection | missing capacity proof | Escalate missing capacity proof when the approved workload exceeds safe capacity or authority is at risk. | Operations |

### Repository evidence boundary

Current foundations are evidenced by the [NestJS module composition](../../apps/api/src/app.module.ts), [Prisma lifecycle](../../apps/api/src/prisma/prisma.service.ts), [PostgreSQL schema and index declarations](../../apps/api/prisma/schema.prisma), [bounded list query DTO](../../apps/api/src/common/list-query.dto.ts), [database health probe](../../apps/api/src/health/health.controller.ts), [report preview](../../apps/api/src/reports/reports.service.ts), [dashboard query service](../../apps/api/src/dashboard/dashboard.service.ts), [Compose topology](../../docker-compose.yml), [accepted migrations](../../apps/api/prisma/migrations), [accepted API tests](../../apps/api/test), and [DBA-002](../implementation/DBA-002-foundation-implementation.md), [DBA-003](../implementation/DBA-003-enterprise-structure-implementation.md) and [DBA-004](../implementation/DBA-004-enterprise-master-data-implementation.md) reports. These artifacts establish implementation foundations only; they contain no representative benchmark, SLO, APM, distributed trace, load harness or approved production capacity envelope.

### Baseline gaps

No accepted evidence shows replicas, distributed cache, CDN, queue, worker, rate limiter, connection proxy, partitioning, sharding, orchestration, multi-region operation, APM, tracing, real-user monitoring, synthetic monitoring, workload forecasting, performance tests, large fixtures, regression gates, mobile synchronization or AI serving. The basic health endpoint proves database reachability at one instant, not readiness under load.

## Chapter 05 — Target Performance Architecture

The target architecture is a control loop, not a product list. Requirements feed workload models, budgets constrain application and data paths, isolation separates interactive, analytical, integration, synchronization and AI demand, and telemetry plus tests provide the evidence used for scaling and release decisions.

### Governed performance contract

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |
|---|---|---|---|
| Requirement | fifteen-layer model | Define fifteen-layer model as a versioned contract with an explicit measurement point. | Performance Architecture |
| Evidence | isolation seams | Supply scale-shaped evidence for isolation seams without substituting an average or anecdote. | Domain owner |
| Protection | evidence feedback | Escalate evidence feedback when the approved workload exceeds safe capacity or authority is at risk. | Operations |

### Fifteen target layers

| Layer | Governing output |
|---:|---|
| 1 | Performance requirements and approval |
| 2 | Versioned workload modeling |
| 3 | Application-path performance |
| 4 | Database plans, locks and maintenance |
| 5 | Caching and eligible read optimization |
| 6 | Asynchronous and bulk execution direction |
| 7 | Integration throughput and backlog control |
| 8 | Mobile/offline synchronization load |
| 9 | Reporting and analytics isolation |
| 10 | Large-data lifecycle patterns |
| 11 | Scaling and capacity decisions |
| 12 | Protected performance observability |
| 13 | Reproducible performance testing |
| 14 | Resilience and recovery under load |
| 15 | Governance, cost and approval |

## Chapter 07 — Performance Budget Architecture

An end-to-end budget apportions user-perceived time among browser work, network, API middleware, authorization and scope resolution, database work, dependencies and serialization. Queue wait, report generation, synchronization and file transfer use separate elapsed-time contracts because hiding them inside server latency would obscure waiting and backlog.

### Governed performance contract

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |
|---|---|---|---|
| Requirement | browser-to-database decomposition | Define browser-to-database decomposition as a versioned contract with an explicit measurement point. | Performance Architecture |
| Evidence | async elapsed time | Supply scale-shaped evidence for async elapsed time without substituting an average or anecdote. | Domain owner |
| Protection | budget breach ownership | Escalate budget breach ownership when the approved workload exceeds safe capacity or authority is at risk. | Operations |

## Chapter 08 — Workload Model Architecture

A workload model is a versioned scenario rather than a single user count. It records tenant distribution, company and plant breadth, active and concurrent actors, read/write mix, seasonality, burst factor, batch windows, payload distribution, dataset cardinality and forecast growth so a result can be replayed.

### Governed performance contract

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |
|---|---|---|---|
| Requirement | tenant and actor mix | Define tenant and actor mix as a versioned contract with an explicit measurement point. | Performance Architecture |
| Evidence | peak and seasonality | Supply scale-shaped evidence for peak and seasonality without substituting an average or anecdote. | Domain owner |
| Protection | versioned dataset | Escalate versioned dataset when the approved workload exceeds safe capacity or authority is at risk. | Operations |

## Chapter 09 — Workload Classes

Interactive transactions, high-frequency operations, bulk jobs, reports, integrations, mobile synchronization, files, background work, AI advice and administration compete differently for CPU, memory, connections, locks and storage. Admission, priority, isolation and degradation therefore attach to the workload class, not merely the endpoint.

### Governed performance contract

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |
|---|---|---|---|
| Requirement | class admission | Define class admission as a versioned contract with an explicit measurement point. | Performance Architecture |
| Evidence | resource competition | Supply scale-shaped evidence for resource competition without substituting an average or anecdote. | Domain owner |
| Protection | priority policy | Escalate priority policy when the approved workload exceeds safe capacity or authority is at risk. | Operations |

## Chapter 10 — Latency and Percentile Objectives

p50 describes the ordinary path, p95 exposes routine contention, and p99 reveals tail behavior important to operational bursts. Every critical journey distinguishes browser-perceived time, API processing, queue delay, database wait and external dependency time, with a bounded timeout and an explicit failure outcome.

### Governed performance contract

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |
|---|---|---|---|

## Chapter 11 — Throughput Architecture

Throughput is expressed in the unit of completed business work: requests, committed transactions, accepted messages, validated rows, delivered files, acknowledged sync commands, completed reports or batch records. A rate is valid only with payload, concurrency, error and consistency conditions stated alongside it.

### Governed performance contract

## Chapter 12 — Concurrency Architecture

Concurrency connects demand to finite resources. The architecture separately caps active users, in-flight HTTP requests, database connections, workers, reports and sync sessions; it observes lock contention and rejects the fiction that all signed-in users execute the same operation simultaneously.

### Governed performance contract

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |

| Requirement | in-flight demand | Define in-flight demand as a versioned contract with an explicit measurement point. | Performance Architecture |

| Protection | resource caps | Escalate resource caps when the approved workload exceeds safe capacity or authority is at risk. | Operations |

## Chapter 13 — Capacity Planning Architecture

Capacity planning compares observed current demand, reproducibly tested capacity and a deliberately lower safe capacity. Headroom covers variability and recovery, growth forecasts include infrastructure lead time, and a named trigger initiates review before a hard saturation boundary is reached.

### Governed performance contract

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |
|---|---|---|---|
| Requirement | safe headroom | Define safe headroom as a versioned contract with an explicit measurement point. | Performance Architecture |
| Evidence | forecast lead time | Supply scale-shaped evidence for forecast lead time without substituting an average or anecdote. | Domain owner |
| Protection | capacity review | Escalate capacity review when the approved workload exceeds safe capacity or authority is at risk. | Operations |

### Architecture views

    Capacityplanning100 --> Capacityplanning101

~~~

**Capacity lifecycle**

~~~mermaid
stateDiagram-v2
    [*] --> ObserveDemand
    ObserveDemand --> TestCapacity: approved workload
    TestCapacity --> DeclareSafeEnvelope: objectives met
    DeclareSafeEnvelope --> ForecastGrowth: reserve recorded
    ForecastGrowth --> CapacityReview: trigger or lead time
    CapacityReview --> OptimizeOrProvision: bottleneck evidence
    OptimizeOrProvision --> ObserveDemand: revalidate
~~~

## Chapter 15 — Multi-Tenant Performance Isolation

Tenant isolation includes resource fairness as well as data filtering. Per-tenant admission, concurrency, report scheduling, bulk throttles and query-cost signals must prevent one large tenant from consuming the shared connection pool or latency budget while preserving explicit commercial and operational policy.

### Governed performance contract

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |

## Chapter 18 — API Payload and Contract Efficiency

Contracts should return only authorized fields required by the journey, use bounded pagination and controlled sorts, and reject pathological filters or payloads. Sparse-field and batch APIs are target options subject to authorization parity; compression cannot excuse unbounded response construction or N+1 relation loading.

### Governed performance contract

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |
|---|---|---|---|
| Requirement | field projection | Define field projection as a versioned contract with an explicit measurement point. | Performance Architecture |
| Evidence | bounded paging | Supply scale-shaped evidence for bounded paging without substituting an average or anecdote. | Domain owner |
| Protection | pathological payload denial | Escalate pathological payload denial when the approved workload exceeds safe capacity or authority is at risk. | Operations |

## Chapter 19 — Web Application Performance

The Next.js application currently uses uncached API fetches for authenticated data and has no accepted real-user monitoring evidence. Target budgets cover route transition, server and client rendering, JavaScript transfer, data fetch, image handling and browser memory, with code splitting and lazy loading chosen from measured bundle and interaction profiles.

## Chapter 20 — Database Performance Architecture

PostgreSQL performance depends on plans, cardinality estimates, statistics, indexes, join order, lock duration, transaction scope, connection pressure and maintenance. The design starts with query shape and data distribution; adding CPU or replicas cannot repair an unselective predicate or an unnecessarily long transaction.

### Governed performance contract

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |
|---|---|---|---|
| Requirement | plan and cardinality | Define plan and cardinality as a versioned contract with an explicit measurement point. | Performance Architecture |
| Evidence | locking and maintenance | Supply scale-shaped evidence for locking and maintenance without substituting an average or anecdote. | Domain owner |
| Protection | growth | Escalate growth when the approved workload exceeds safe capacity or authority is at risk. | Operations |

### Architecture views

**DB query path**

~~~mermaid
flowchart LR

## Chapter 21 — Database Indexing Strategy

Indexes serve named access paths: primary and unique constraints protect identity, composite order follows selective predicates and sort needs, and prospective partial or covering indexes require plan evidence. Every added index carries write amplification, storage and maintenance cost that is reviewed against an observed query fingerprint.

### Governed performance contract

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |
|---|---|---|---|
| Requirement | access-path evidence | Define access-path evidence as a versioned contract with an explicit measurement point. | Performance Architecture |
| Evidence | selectivity | Supply scale-shaped evidence for selectivity without substituting an average or anecdote. | Domain owner |
| Protection | write amplification | Escalate write amplification when the approved workload exceeds safe capacity or authority is at risk. | Operations |

## Chapter 24 — Database Connection Management

Each API instance multiplies its database pool, so scale-out can exhaust PostgreSQL before CPU saturates. Pool size, acquisition timeout, idle behavior, request admission and prospective proxy use must be designed together; a connection held during external I/O is treated as a correctness and capacity defect.

### Governed performance contract

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |
|---|---|---|---|
| Requirement | pool multiplication | Define pool multiplication as a versioned contract with an explicit measurement point. | Performance Architecture |
| Evidence | acquisition wait | Supply scale-shaped evidence for acquisition wait without substituting an average or anecdote. | Domain owner |
| Protection | proxy direction | Escalate proxy direction when the approved workload exceeds safe capacity or authority is at risk. | Operations |

### Architecture views

    Connectionpoolmodel241["Per-instance pool"]

    Connectionpoolmodel242 --> Connectionpoolmodel243
    Connectionpoolmodel243 --> Connectionpoolmodel244
~~~

**Connection exhaustion**

~~~mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Requests
    participant ApiPool
    participant Postgres
    participant Operations
    Requests->>ApiPool: acquire connection
    ApiPool->>Postgres: within global budget
    Postgres-->>ApiPool: ceiling pressure
    ApiPool-->>Requests: bounded wait or controlled reject
    ApiPool->>Operations: pool wait and timeout alert
    Operations->>ApiPool: reduce admission and preserve reserve
~~~

## Chapter 25 — Read Scaling Direction

Read replicas are a future direction for eligible analytical or explicitly stale reads, not present infrastructure. Routing must preserve read-after-write semantics, keep authoritative validation and writes on the primary, expose replication lag, and define failover rather than silently returning old finance or stock state.

## Chapter 29 — Application Cache Direction

An in-process cache trades network latency for per-instance inconsistency, while a distributed cache adds availability and isolation obligations. Configuration and master-data candidates require revision-aware invalidation; permission caching is higher risk because stale grants can become an authorization bypass.

## Chapter 32 — Bulk Processing Architecture

Bulk work is chunked, checkpointed, resumable and throttled against an explicit resource budget. Validation errors are isolated by record, partial completion is visible, and administrative mass change never gets an unlimited transaction that monopolizes locks or conceals which rows committed.

## Chapter 36 — Retry Storm and Backpressure Control

Backpressure tells callers when safe capacity is unavailable; retries use exponential delay, jitter and a finite budget. Admission control, circuit-breaker direction and load shedding protect dependencies, while queue depth remains a business backlog with age and ownership instead of a mechanism that hides failure.

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |

sequenceDiagram

    participant Admission
    participant Dependency
    participant Recovery

    Admission->>Dependency: bounded attempt
    Dependency-->>Admission: unavailable
    Admission-->>Partner: retry-after plus jitter budget
    Recovery->>Admission: dependency health proven
    Admission->>Dependency: paced recovery cohort
    Dependency-->>Partner: reconciled outcome
~~~

## Chapter 53 — Autoscaling Direction

Autoscaling is a future control loop defined by metric, threshold, hysteresis, cooldown, minimum, maximum, state safety and cost ceiling. It is not present and would not compensate for inefficient SQL, unbounded payloads, connection multiplication or a stateful local-file design.

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |

## Chapter 54 — Graceful Degradation

Graceful degradation protects core posting, stock, production and service transactions by delaying heavy reports, optional AI, derivatives and nonessential media. Every degradation is visible and reversible; authorization, audit, consistency and confirmation are never weakened, and an unsuccessful transaction is never presented as completed.

## Chapter 63 — Capability, Risk, Example and Governance Models

The governance catalogs turn the preceding architecture into auditable ownership: capabilities distinguish evidence from targets, risks name concrete failure mechanisms, examples bind workloads to verification, ADRs record consequences, open decisions demand specific evidence, and the RACI separates accountability from execution.

### Governed performance contract

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |
|---|---|---|---|
| Requirement | catalog integration | Define catalog integration as a versioned contract with an explicit measurement point. | Performance Architecture |
| Evidence | evidence statuses | Supply scale-shaped evidence for evidence statuses without substituting an average or anecdote. | Domain owner |
| Protection | separated ownership | Escalate separated ownership when the approved workload exceeds safe capacity or authority is at risk. | Operations |

### Performance risk register

| Risk ID | Performance area | Risk | Current condition | Impact | Target mitigation | Owner | Residual-risk direction |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PR-001 | Performance requirements | Missing workload model | A latency or capacity target is approved without actor mix, concurrent requests, transaction proportions, payload distribution, dataset size or peak factor; a light test can therefore appear successful. Evidence for missing workload model records the triggering workload, first affected journey and constrained resource; trend missing workload model by tenant and workload phase, and correlate missing workload model onset with missing workload model recovery. | Infrastructure may be sized and a release accepted on evidence that collapses under the real business mix, leaving tail latency and recovery headroom unknown. For missing workload model, count affected transactions, incomplete or incorrect business outcomes and recovery duration. The missing workload model owner reconciles every missing workload model exception before closing the missing workload model event. | Make the target invalid until a versioned workload contract, domain-owner approval, governed benchmark dataset and replayable scenario are attached. Exercise missing workload model under the named workload, retain pre/post metrics and reconcile affected work. Accept the missing workload model control only when repeated missing workload model trials remain inside the objective and missing workload model recovery is owned. | Performance Architecture | Falls only after the approved workload is replayed and capacity is recalculated from the representative result. |

### Performance example catalog

Scale terms are governed workload assumptions, not production claims.

| Example ID | Example | Primary owner | Workload class | Trigger | Expected scale | Performance objective | Bottleneck risk | Scaling response direction | Degradation behavior | Evidence required | Reconciliation/verification | Specific risk | Current status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PE-001 | User login peak | Security | Interactive transactional | A shift or business-day start produces a concurrent login burst requiring credential lookup, JWT issue, permission expansion and organization-scope loading. | Versioned login cohort with active-request distribution, role-assignment depth and tenant/company breadth. | Authentication p95/p99, successful logins/second, denial accuracy, database time and pool wait remain inside the approved login budget. | Permission graph loading and organization-scope queries can hold database connections across the cohort. | Bound login admission and abuse attempts, optimize only measured permission/scope queries, and preserve connection reserve.; For user login peak, the scaling owner records which measured bottleneck the user login peak action relieves and retests user login peak without changing its workload. | Return controlled retry guidance for excess demand; never skip authentication, disabled-user, MFA direction or permission checks.; During user login peak, expose pending or rejected state explicitly; close degradation only after the user login peak backlog and authoritative user login peak outcomes reconcile. | Replayable login script, role/scope dataset, stage timings, pool use, success/denial counts and tail samples. | Security reconciles successful and rejected identities; Platform Engineering confirms no duplicate sessions or bypass. | A fast-path permission cache could outlive revocation or collide across tenants. The user login peak risk is accepted only against the versioned user login peak shape; a smaller user login peak replay can conceal the exact fan-out, contention or fairness limit. | Partial |
| PE-002 | Dashboard morning peak | Reporting | Reporting | Many users request the same tenant/company dashboard metrics immediately after login, repeating counts and aggregates within one refresh window. | User cohort by tenant/company, dashboard mix, drill-down ratio and controlled staleness window. | Dashboard render p95/p99, database aggregate time, repeated-query count and OLTP latency remain within budget. | Identical aggregate queries can multiply across users and compete with transactional reads. | Coalesce identical scoped requests, govern refresh cadence and reuse only eligible aggregates with a source watermark.; The dashboard morning peak response is authorized only from a causal dashboard morning peak trace; capacity added for dashboard morning peak must improve the named outcome rather than shift pressure downstream. | Serve the last approved metric snapshot with visible staleness; drill-down always reads an authoritative permitted path.; If dashboard morning peak exceeds admission, preserve the core transaction and defer only the listed optional work. Recovery ends when dashboard morning peak receipts and dashboard morning peak business totals agree. | Morning-cohort replay, query fingerprints, aggregate reuse ratio, snapshot age and concurrent OLTP results. | Reporting certifies metric meaning; Database Engineering verifies query reduction and authoritative drill-down. | Unlabeled cached metrics can appear current after source data changes. A dashboard morning peak benchmark is misleading if it removes the cohort, skew or authority checks that make dashboard morning peak expensive. Retain the full dashboard morning peak shape in every dashboard morning peak comparison. | Partial |
| PE-003 | Large customer list | Sales | Interactive transactional | A user filters and sorts a high-cardinality tenant/company customer set and navigates beyond the first pages while a count is requested. | Skewed customer dataset, selective and nonselective filters, supported sorts, page depth and payload width. | List p95/p99, rows scanned/returned, count duration, response bytes and pool wait stay bounded. | Deep offset, wildcard search or unsupported sort can force scan/sort work that grows with customer count. | Enforce projection and page limits; compare bounded offset with cursor/continuation using actual plans before selecting a policy.; Before scaling large customer list, compare optimization and admission options against the large customer list resource signature and preserve a rollback point for large customer list. | Reject unsafe filters or return a bounded page with continuation; never broaden company scope.; The large customer list fallback must remain truthful about stale, queued or failed work. Restore the normal path only after large customer list recovery and large customer list authority checks pass. | Query fingerprints/plans, page-depth matrix, response bytes, count cost and tenant-scope verification. | Sales validates returned customers and sort semantics; Database Engineering verifies plans and scan efficiency. | A fast count or search path may omit tenant/company predicates. Changing data, concurrency or optional controls can make large customer list appear healthy without increasing real large customer list capacity; every large customer list result therefore carries its workload hash. | Partial |
| PE-004 | Large item list | Data Governance | Interactive transactional | A user requests large item list over the approved high-cardinality, skewed dataset with realistic filters, sorts and scope. | Versioned large item list workload with tenant/company masters, hierarchy depth, filter selectivity and payload width; all quantities remain benchmark inputs. | For large item list, measure list/search p95/p99, rows scanned/returned, bytes and query count against its approved journey or completion budget. | unselective filters or relationship depth can expand database work | bound projection/filter/page shape and optimize only evidenced queries; Treat large item list as its own capacity decision: the large item list owner links the action to the saturated component and repeats the full large item list reconciliation. | return bounded continuation or defer enrichment; preserve scope; Overload handling for large item list keeps authorization, audit and confirmation intact. The large item list incident remains active until all large item list accepted work reaches a reconciled state. | Replayable large item list script, governed dataset, arrival distribution, raw percentiles/rates, errors and correlated resource series. | Data Governance validates records; Database Engineering validates plans | If large item list omits tenant/company masters, hierarchy depth, filter selectivity and payload width, the benchmark can pass while its actual contention and authority path remain untested. | PE-030 | Attachment download | Platform Engineering | Administrative/runtime | The attachment download workload begins with concurrent large artifacts and includes interrupted transfer, resume and access-control paths. | Versioned attachment download workload with instances, route mix, pools, dependencies and restart/scale state; all quantities remain benchmark inputs. | For attachment download, measure readiness, pool wait, error rate, p95/p99 and resource saturation against its approved journey or completion budget. | cold start, local state or pool multiplication can shift bottlenecks | verify the bottleneck, readiness and downstream budget before action; The attachment download response is authorized only from a causal attachment download trace; capacity added for attachment download must improve the named outcome rather than shift pressure downstream. | reject optional work and drain safely; preserve core routes; If attachment download exceeds admission, preserve the core transaction and defer only the listed optional work. Recovery ends when attachment download receipts and attachment download business totals agree. | Replayable attachment download script, governed dataset, arrival distribution, raw percentiles/rates, errors and correlated resource series. | Platform/Operations reconcile active work and runtime state | If attachment download omits instances, route mix, pools, dependencies and restart/scale state, the benchmark can pass while its actual contention and authority path remain untested. Retain the full attachment download shape in every attachment download comparison. | Conceptual target architecture |
| PE-031 | Large audit query | Data Governance | Bulk/batch | A user requests large audit query over the approved high-cardinality, skewed dataset with realistic filters, sorts and scope. | Versioned large audit query workload with retention classes, row/file growth, legal hold and retrieval demand; all quantities remain benchmark inputs. | For large audit query, measure scan/retrieval duration, bytes, IOPS and OLTP impact against its approved journey or completion budget. | unbounded history or lifecycle work can consume hot storage/I/O | classify lifecycle and use bounded, reconciled chunks; Before scaling large audit query, compare optimization and admission options against the large audit query resource signature and preserve a rollback point for large audit query. | throttle/checkpoint; preserve legal hold and retrieval evidence; The large audit query fallback must remain truthful about stale, queued or failed work. Restore the normal path only after large audit query recovery and large audit query authority checks pass. | Replayable large audit query script, governed dataset, arrival distribution, raw percentiles/rates, errors and correlated resource series. | Data Governance reconciles retained, archived and purged records | If large audit query omits retention classes, row/file growth, legal hold and retrieval demand, the benchmark can pass while its actual contention and authority path remain untested. Changing data, concurrency or optional controls can make large audit query appear healthy without increasing real large audit query capacity; every large audit query result therefore carries its workload hash. | Conceptual target architecture |
| PE-032 | Archive retrieval | Data Governance | Bulk/batch | The approved archive retrieval transaction mix reaches its modeled concurrency and data-shape boundary. | Versioned archive retrieval workload with retention classes, row/file growth, legal hold and retrieval demand; all quantities remain benchmark inputs. | For archive retrieval, measure scan/retrieval duration, bytes, IOPS and OLTP impact against its approved journey or completion budget. | unbounded history or lifecycle work can consume hot storage/I/O | classify lifecycle and use bounded, reconciled chunks; Treat archive retrieval as its own capacity decision: the archive retrieval owner links the action to the saturated component and repeats the full archive retrieval reconciliation. | throttle/checkpoint; preserve legal hold and retrieval evidence; Overload handling for archive retrieval keeps authorization, audit and confirmation intact. | PE-034 | Database connection pressure | Database Engineering | Interactive transactional | An API request burst or planned scale-out increases per-instance pool demand until the global PostgreSQL connection budget approaches its ceiling. | API instance counts, per-instance pool limits, route concurrency, transaction duration and reserved operational connections. | Pool utilization/wait p95/p99, acquisition timeout rate, reserved capacity and journey latency remain within policy. | Instance multiplication can exhaust connections before API CPU saturates, causing waits and failed health/business requests. | Enforce global/per-instance budgets, acquisition timeout, pool-wait alerts and draining; justify a proxy only with measured evidence.; The database connection pressure response is authorized only from a causal database connection pressure trace; capacity added for database connection pressure must improve the named outcome rather than shift pressure downstream. | Reject excess optional work with retry guidance while preserving admin/incident reserve; do not add instances blindly.; If database connection pressure exceeds admission, preserve the core transaction and defer only the listed optional work. Recovery ends when database connection pressure receipts and database connection pressure business totals agree. | All-instance concurrency replay, pool/DB connection series, timeout taxonomy and scale/drain verification. | Database Engineering reconciles potential/actual connections; Operations verifies reserve and recovery. | A scale action can improve API CPU while making the database bottleneck worse. A database connection pressure benchmark is misleading if it removes the cohort, skew or authority checks that make database connection pressure expensive. Retain the full database connection pressure shape in every database connection pressure comparison. | Partial |
| PE-035 | Cache cold start | Platform Engineering | Background and interactive reads | An application restart or eviction event leaves eligible cache classes empty while normal requests simultaneously demand those values. | Key-class popularity, tenant distribution, request concurrency, source-query cost and staged warmup set. | Miss-load p95/p99, simultaneous loaders, database contribution, hit-ratio recovery and core-transaction impact meet limits. | Demand fill or aggressive warmup can launch many identical source queries and consume database reserve. | Use single-flight demand loading and staged key-family warmup with loader concurrency capped against database headroom.; Before scaling cache cold start, compare optimization and admission options against the cache cold start resource signature and preserve a rollback point for cache cold start. | Bypass only within the source-query budget; defer nonessential warmup and keep authoritative reads correct.; The cache cold start fallback must remain truthful about stale, queued or failed work. Restore the normal path only after cache cold start recovery and cache cold start authority checks pass. | Cold-start replay, loader concurrency, source fingerprints, hit/miss timeline and concurrent OLTP results. | Platform verifies cache behavior; Database Engineering confirms source reserve; domains verify value revisions. | Warmup order can favor one tenant or hot key and violate fairness. Changing data, concurrency or optional controls can make cache cold start appear healthy without increasing real cache cold start capacity; every cache cold start result therefore carries its workload hash. | Future |

The prisma pool acquisition risk is accepted only against the versioned prisma pool acquisition shape; a smaller prisma pool acquisition replay can conceal the exact fan-out, contention or fairness limit. | Conceptual target architecture |
| PE-150 | Health-probe surge | Platform Engineering | Administrative/runtime | The approved health-probe surge transaction mix reaches its modeled concurrency and data-shape boundary. | Versioned health-probe surge workload with instances, route mix, pools, dependencies and restart/scale state; all quantities remain benchmark inputs. | For health-probe surge, measure readiness, pool wait, error rate, p95/p99 and resource saturation against its approved journey or completion budget. | cold start, local state or pool multiplication can shift bottlenecks | verify the bottleneck, readiness and downstream budget before action; The health-probe surge response is authorized only from a causal health-probe surge trace; capacity added for health-probe surge must improve the named outcome rather than shift pressure downstream. | reject optional work and drain safely; preserve core routes; If health-probe surge exceeds admission, preserve the core transaction and defer only the listed optional work. Recovery ends when health-probe surge receipts and health-probe surge business totals agree. | Replayable health-probe surge script, governed dataset, arrival distribution, raw percentiles/rates, errors and correlated resource series. | Platform/Operations reconcile active work and runtime state | If health-probe surge omits instances, route mix, pools, dependencies and restart/scale state, the benchmark can pass while its actual contention and authority path remain untested. A health-probe surge benchmark is misleading if it removes the cohort, skew or authority checks that make health-probe surge expensive. Retain the full health-probe surge shape in every health-probe surge comparison. | Conceptual target architecture |

### Current-versus-target evidence matrix

| Subject | Current evidence-based state | Target direction | Repository evidence or absence basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| API topology | One NestJS API service in Compose | Stateless scale-ready API direction with explicit external state and pool budget | [Compose](../../docker-compose.yml); [AppModule](../../apps/api/src/app.module.ts) |
| Web topology | One Next.js web service in Compose | Measured web budgets and scale direction | [Compose](../../docker-compose.yml); [web package](../../apps/web/package.json) |
| Database | PostgreSQL through Prisma | Capacity-evidenced primary plus optional approved read isolation | [schema](../../apps/api/prisma/schema.prisma) |
| Pagination | Common DTO defaults to 20 and caps at 100 | Journey-specific bounded pagination and continuation policy | [ListQueryDto](../../apps/api/src/common/list-query.dto.ts) |
| Indexes | Accepted primary, unique and composite declarations | Fingerprint-driven index lifecycle | [schema](../../apps/api/prisma/schema.prisma); [migrations](../../apps/api/prisma/migrations) |
| Health | Database `SELECT 1` returns service status | Readiness, saturation and dependency health model | [health controller](../../apps/api/src/health/health.controller.ts) |
| Prisma lifecycle | One client object per process lifecycle | Approved pool and acquisition policy across instance count | [PrismaService](../../apps/api/src/prisma/prisma.service.ts) |
| Reporting | Definitions and 50-row transaction preview | Classified, cancellable, isolated heavy-report execution | [ReportsService](../../apps/api/src/reports/reports.service.ts) |
| Dashboards | Parallel counts, aggregates and relation-heavy finance summary | Budgeted refresh, aggregate reuse and labeled staleness | [DashboardService](../../apps/api/src/dashboard/dashboard.service.ts) |
| Tests | Functional accepted suites | Versioned load, stress, spike, soak, volume and regression suites | [API tests](../../apps/api/test) |
| Cache | No accepted dependency or runtime | Authorization-aware reconstructible cache if evidence justifies | [API package](../../apps/api/package.json) |
| Queue and workers | No accepted queue or background worker | Durable idempotent job direction with backlog ownership | [API package](../../apps/api/package.json) |
| Read replicas | No accepted replication topology | Eligible stale/reporting read direction after lag contract | [Compose](../../docker-compose.yml) |
| Partitioning | No accepted partition DDL | Measured table/key candidate with migration rehearsal | [migrations](../../apps/api/prisma/migrations) |
| Sharding | No accepted shard router or data placement | Deferred evidence-triggered direction | [schema](../../apps/api/prisma/schema.prisma) |
| Observability | Application logs and audit trace IDs only | Protected metrics, structured logs and prospective tracing | [AuditService](../../apps/api/src/audit/audit.service.ts) |
| Autoscaling | No orchestration or autoscaling runtime | Future metric/hysteresis/cooldown policy after stateless proof | [Compose](../../docker-compose.yml) |
| Mobile sync | No accepted mobile or synchronization runtime | FCSB-022 bounded sync and reconnect load direction | [FCSB-022](./FCSB-Volume-22-Mobile-and-Offline-Architecture.md) |
| AI serving | No accepted model-serving runtime | Optional isolated advisory capacity direction | [API package](../../apps/api/package.json) |
| Performance governance | No formal budgets, workload registry or gates | Versioned evidence and Architecture Board approval lifecycle | [Series Index](./FCSB-Series-Index.md) |

### Architecture decision record register

| ADR ID | Decision | Status | Performance-specific rationale and rejected unsafe alternative | Accountable owner | Evidence before implementation |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ADR-023-001 | Workload model precedes performance target | Proposed | Workload model precedes performance target because targets are meaningful only for an actor, transaction, concurrency and data distribution. Reusing an average or light scenario is rejected: it can certify capacity for traffic the business will never produce. This protects domain-approved workload semantics. Reviewers may ratify the 'workload model precedes performance target' decision only from its listed evidence; the 'workload model precedes performance target' record retains the rejected alternative and a measurable reversal trigger. | Performance Architecture | Versioned workload contract, domain approvals, dataset profile, arrival curve and replay result. |
| ADR-023-002 | Percentile latency is required for critical journeys | Proposed | Percentile latency is required for critical journeys so tail wait and completed business work remain visible across browser, API, queue and database boundaries. An average or server-only measure is rejected because it hides slow cohorts and uncompleted work at scale. This protects the approved journey budget. Approval of the 'percentile latency is required for critical journeys' decision names its operating owner, acceptance boundary and rollback point. A future change to 'percentile latency is required for critical journeys' must replay the same evidence before supersession. | Performance Architecture | Critical-journey samples, p50/p95/p99, throughput unit, wait decomposition and timeout outcomes. |
| ADR-023-003 | Performance budgets are end-to-end | Proposed | Performance budgets are end-to-end so tail wait and completed business work remain visible across browser, API, queue and database boundaries. An average or server-only measure is rejected because it hides slow cohorts and uncompleted work at scale. This protects the approved journey budget. The decision log for 'performance budgets are end-to-end' links workload assumptions to the selected control and states when that choice must be revisited; repository delivery alone does not approve 'performance budgets are end-to-end'. | Performance Architecture | Critical-journey samples, p50/p95/p99, throughput unit, wait decomposition and timeout outcomes. |
| ADR-023-004 | Domain owners define workload semantics | Proposed | Domain owners define workload semantics because targets are meaningful only for an actor, transaction, concurrency and data distribution. Reusing an average or light scenario is rejected: it can certify capacity for traffic the business will never produce. This protects domain-approved workload semantics. For the 'domain owners define workload semantics' decision, the accountable owner certifies the measurement boundary and residual trade-off. The 'domain owners define workload semantics' rationale expires when its workload or authority assumptions change. | Performance Architecture | Versioned workload contract, domain approvals, dataset profile, arrival curve and replay result. |
| ADR-023-005 | Operations owns runtime capacity | Proposed | Operations owns runtime capacity because production saturation, telemetry and scaling execution require one operational authority. Architecture-driven runtime changes are rejected because policy ownership does not provide incident context or execution accountability. Domain semantics remain outside Operations. Reviewers may ratify the 'operations owns runtime capacity' decision only from its listed evidence; the 'operations owns runtime capacity' record retains the rejected alternative and a measurable reversal trigger. | Operations | Operational runbook, capacity dashboard, incident roles, change record and post-action verification. |
| ADR-023-006 | Database Engineering owns DB capacity and tuning | Proposed | Database Engineering owns DB capacity and tuning because plans, write amplification, lock duration and the global connection ceiling are database-wide constraints. Endpoint-local tuning or adding API instances is rejected because it can move pressure into PostgreSQL and harm every domain. Database Engineering retains tuning and pool authority. Approval of the 'database engineering owns db capacity and tuning' decision names its operating owner, acceptance boundary and rollback point. A future change to 'database engineering owns db capacity and tuning' must replay the same evidence before supersession. | Database Engineering | Query fingerprint/plan, before-after latency and write cost, pool budget, lock evidence and rollback trigger. |
| ADR-023-007 | Caching never becomes system of record | Proposed | Caching never becomes system of record establishes a named performance control with accountable evidence. An informal convention is rejected because it cannot be tested, operated or audited consistently as workload grows. Performance Architecture retains the standard while the mechanism owner executes it. The decision log for 'caching never becomes system of record' links workload assumptions to the selected control and states when that choice must be revisited; repository delivery alone does not approve 'caching never becomes system of record'. | Performance Architecture | Decision-specific workload, measurement boundary, owner, failure/recovery result and approval record. |
| ADR-023-008 | Authorization-aware cache boundaries are mandatory | Proposed | Authorization-aware cache boundaries are mandatory because cached values must remain reconstructible, scope-safe and revision-aware. Treating cache as authoritative or keying only by record ID is rejected because stale permissions or cross-tenant results can be served quickly but incorrectly. Security and domains retain authority. For the 'authorization-aware cache boundaries are mandatory' decision, the accountable owner certifies the measurement boundary and residual trade-off. The 'authorization-aware cache boundaries are mandatory' rationale expires when its workload or authority assumptions change. | Platform Engineering | Eligibility/staleness class, scoped key design, invalidation latency, collision, expiry, bypass and stampede tests. |
| ADR-023-009 | Reporting workloads must not starve OLTP | Proposed | Reporting workloads must not starve OLTP because targets are meaningful only for an actor, transaction, concurrency and data distribution. Reusing an average or light scenario is rejected: it can certify capacity for traffic the business will never produce. This protects domain-approved workload semantics. Reviewers may ratify the 'reporting workloads must not starve oltp' decision only from its listed evidence; the 'reporting workloads must not starve oltp' record retains the rejected alternative and a measurable reversal trigger. | Performance Architecture | Versioned workload contract, domain approvals, dataset profile, arrival curve and replay result. |
| ADR-023-010 | Bulk jobs are throttled | Proposed | Bulk jobs are throttled because noninteractive work still needs idempotency, checkpoints, ownership and visible completion. One long synchronous transaction or opaque queue is rejected because overload and partial effects become unrecoverable at scale. The domain retains the business outcome. Approval of the 'bulk jobs are throttled' decision names its operating owner, acceptance boundary and rollback point. A future change to 'bulk jobs are throttled' must replay the same evidence before supersession. | Platform Engineering | Chunk/transaction size, checkpoint/resume, duplicate test, oldest age, cancellation and source-to-commit reconciliation. |
| ADR-023-011 | Retries use bounded backoff | Proposed | Retries use bounded backoff establishes a named performance control with accountable evidence. An informal convention is rejected because it cannot be tested, operated or audited consistently as workload grows. Performance Architecture retains the standard while the mechanism owner executes it. The decision log for 'retries use bounded backoff' links workload assumptions to the selected control and states when that choice must be revisited; repository delivery alone does not approve 'retries use bounded backoff'. | Performance Architecture | Decision-specific workload, measurement boundary, owner, failure/recovery result and approval record. |
| ADR-023-012 | Retry storms must be prevented | Proposed | Retry storms must be prevented because failure recovery can amplify partner traffic beyond both systems' capacity. Immediate or unlimited replay is rejected because synchronized callers can break a recovering dependency again. Integration owns retry/backoff and backlog policy. For the 'retry storms must be prevented' decision, the accountable owner certifies the measurement boundary and residual trade-off. The 'retry storms must be prevented' rationale expires when its workload or authority assumptions change. | Integration | Partner rate/burst, amplification ratio, jitter distribution, retry exhaustion, oldest age and recovery reconciliation. |
| ADR-023-013 | Mobile reconnects are paced | Proposed | Mobile reconnects are paced because returning devices create correlated command, delta and receipt load. Unbounded reconnect or payload replay is rejected because it overwhelms server admission and violates device/tenant fairness. Mobile owns client pacing; Platform owns server admission. Reviewers may ratify the 'mobile reconnects are paced' decision only from its listed evidence; the 'mobile reconnects are paced' record retains the rejected alternative and a measurable reversal trigger. | Mobile Engineering | Device cohorts, jitter, commands/device, batch/delta bytes, receipt p99, conflict rate and backlog drain. |
| ADR-023-014 | Sync uses bounded batches | Proposed | Sync uses bounded batches because returning devices create correlated command, delta and receipt load. An informal convention is rejected because it cannot be tested, operated or audited consistently as workload grows. Performance Architecture retains the standard while the mechanism owner executes it. | ADR-023-016 | Horizontal scaling requires explicit state handling | Proposed | Horizontal scaling requires explicit state handling because instance changes are safe only when local state, active work and database-pool multiplication are controlled. Blind scale-out or abrupt termination is rejected because it can worsen the database bottleneck or lose work. Operations executes scaling; Platform proves readiness. For the 'horizontal scaling requires explicit state handling' decision, the accountable owner certifies the measurement boundary and residual trade-off. The 'horizontal scaling requires explicit state handling' rationale expires when its workload or authority assumptions change. | Operations | State inventory, readiness, global pool budget, scale/drain times, active-work reconciliation and post-scale capacity. |
| ADR-023-017 | Autoscaling does not replace optimization | Proposed | Autoscaling does not replace optimization because automatic instance changes must respond to sustained causal signals with hysteresis and cooldown. A sensitive single-metric loop is rejected because it oscillates, disrupts active requests and expands connection/cost demand. Operations retains runtime authority. Reviewers may ratify the 'autoscaling does not replace optimization' decision only from its listed evidence; the 'autoscaling does not replace optimization' record retains the rejected alternative and a measurable reversal trigger. | Operations | Metric/outcome correlation, thresholds, hysteresis, cooldown, connection ceiling, oscillation and drain tests. |
| ADR-023-018 | Sharding is evidence-driven and deferred until justified | Deferred | Sharding is evidence-driven and deferred until justified because sharding changes routing, identity, cross-shard query and recovery semantics. Premature distribution is rejected because it multiplies failure modes before a single-database limit is proven. Architecture approves the threshold; Database Engineering owns any future mechanism. Approval of the 'sharding is evidence-driven and deferred until justified' decision names its operating owner, acceptance boundary and rollback point. A future change to 'sharding is evidence-driven and deferred until justified' must replay the same evidence before supersession. | Database Engineering | Single-database saturation, rejected tuning options, placement model, routing, rebalance, failover and operating skills. |
| ADR-023-019 | Partitioning requires measurable benefit | Proposed | Partitioning requires measurable benefit because partitioning adds key, pruning, maintenance and migration obligations. Partitioning by convention is rejected because skew or unpruned queries can increase operational cost without relieving the measured bottleneck. Database Engineering owns the mechanism. The decision log for 'partitioning requires measurable benefit' links workload assumptions to the selected control and states when that choice must be revisited; repository delivery alone does not approve 'partitioning requires measurable benefit'. | Database Engineering | Table growth, plans, pruning rate, key skew, maintenance rehearsal, migration rollback and staffing. |
| ADR-023-020 | Read replicas cannot serve authoritative writes | Proposed | Read replicas cannot serve authoritative writes because replication lag makes read freshness a business property, not merely a routing optimization. Database Engineering controls routing mechanisms; domains approve staleness. For the 'read replicas cannot serve authoritative writes' decision, the accountable owner certifies the measurement boundary and residual trade-off. | OD-023-001 | Performance-budget model | Performance Architecture | Named journeys and owners; actor/transaction workload; p50/p95/p99 and timeout; browser/API/database/dependency allocation; breach and exception policy. The reviewer must be able to reproduce the signed url lifetime measurement, trace the signed url lifetime authority boundary and verify the signed url lifetime recovery behavior. | File/edge architecture cannot be selected until access and lifecycle behavior are testable. | Before storage/CDN selection |
| OD-023-076 | Job idempotency standard | Platform Engineering | For Job idempotency standard: job eligibility, business owner, enqueue/dequeue/completion rate, oldest age, idempotency, retries, priority, dead-letter disposition, worker failure and recovery. Close job idempotency standard only with a versioned job idempotency standard decision record, quantified pass/fail boundary and an owner for the residual job idempotency standard risk. | Queue/worker runtime remains Future because accepted work lacks a governed completion and failure model. | Before queue/worker selection |
| OD-023-077 | Dead-letter ownership | Platform Engineering | For Dead-letter ownership: job eligibility, business owner, enqueue/dequeue/completion rate, oldest age, idempotency, retries, priority, dead-letter disposition, worker failure and recovery. The dead-letter ownership packet must compare options, name the selected dead-letter ownership limit and record a reversible dead-letter ownership acceptance test. | Queue/worker runtime remains Future because accepted work lacks a governed completion and failure model. | Before queue/worker selection |
| OD-023-078 | Job priority model | Platform Engineering | Named priority classes and owning domains; admission share; per-tenant fairness; maximum wait and starvation threshold; priority inversion test; reserved core-business capacity; override authority and expiry; recovery ordering. Evidence for job priority model also identifies the operating owner, rejected alternative and the trigger that reopens the job priority model decision. | A single FIFO or informal priority convention could let bulk work starve finance, stock, production or customer-facing completion. | Before implementation architecture approval |
| OD-023-079 | Bulk checkpoint format | Platform Engineering | Stable job and source identity; last durable source offset or key range; checksum and schema version; committed/rejected counters; idempotency token; restart owner; atomic checkpoint write; corrupted-checkpoint handling and replay proof. The reviewer must be able to reproduce the bulk checkpoint format measurement, trace the bulk checkpoint format authority boundary and verify the bulk checkpoint format recovery behavior. | A restart could skip or duplicate rows because no durable checkpoint is tied to the exact source and committed effects. | Before implementation architecture approval |
| OD-023-080 | Import cancellation semantics | Platform Engineering | Cancellation states before validation, between commit chunks and after final commit; atomic chunk boundary; in-flight transaction handling; checkpoint durability; resumable versus terminal status; user-visible accepted/rejected counts; authorization and audit of cancel/resume. Close import cancellation semantics only with a versioned import cancellation semantics decision record, quantified pass/fail boundary and an owner for the residual import cancellation semantics risk. | A user could believe an import stopped while additional chunks commit, or restart it and duplicate already accepted rows. | Before implementation architecture approval |
| OD-023-081 | Export snapshot semantics | Reporting | Authoritative selection timestamp or source watermark; transaction-isolation choice; treatment of rows changing during generation; page/chunk ordering; totals and control hash; stale-label rule; reconciliation back to source and certified-report requirements. The export snapshot semantics packet must compare options, name the selected export snapshot semantics limit and record a reversible export snapshot semantics acceptance test. | A multi-chunk export could contain internally inconsistent rows and totals even when each query succeeds. | Before implementation architecture approval |
| OD-023-082 | Partner burst allowance | Integration | For Partner burst allowance: partner sustained/burst rate, retry amplification, backoff/jitter, idempotency, backlog oldest age, circuit probes, throttled responses and recovery reconciliation. Evidence for partner burst allowance also identifies the operating owner, rejected alternative and the trigger that reopens the partner burst allowance decision. | Partner recovery could re-saturate both systems without an approved envelope and ownership. | Before partner throughput agreement |
| OD-023-083 | Circuit-breaker adoption | Integration | For Circuit-breaker adoption: partner sustained/burst rate, retry amplification, backoff/jitter, idempotency, backlog oldest age, circuit probes, throttled responses and recovery reconciliation. The reviewer must be able to reproduce the circuit-breaker adoption measurement, trace the circuit-breaker adoption authority boundary and verify the circuit-breaker adoption recovery behavior. | Partner recovery could re-saturate both systems without an approved envelope and ownership. | Before partner throughput agreement |
| OD-023-084 | Reconnection cohort size | Mobile Engineering | For Reconnection cohort size: devices by tenant, offline duration, jitter/cohorts, commands/device, delta rows/bytes, receipt p99, conflict rate, media deferral and backlog drain. Close reconnection cohort size only with a versioned reconnection cohort size decision record, quantified pass/fail boundary and an owner for the residual reconnection cohort size risk. | Mobile synchronization remains Future because reconnect and payload capacity are not established. | Before mobile synchronization implementation |
| OD-023-085 | Sync delta ceiling | Mobile Engineering | For Sync delta ceiling: devices by tenant, offline duration, jitter/cohorts, commands/device, delta rows/bytes, receipt p99, conflict rate, media deferral and backlog drain. The sync delta ceiling packet must compare options, name the selected sync delta ceiling limit and record a reversible sync delta ceiling acceptance test. | Mobile synchronization remains Future because reconnect and payload capacity are not established. | Before mobile synchronization implementation |
| OD-023-086 | Warehouse scan objective | Performance Architecture | For Warehouse scan objective: domain-approved actors, active/concurrent distribution, transaction mix, payload/data shape, tenant/company/plant breadth, sustained/peak curve and replay script. Evidence for warehouse scan objective also identifies the operating owner, rejected alternative and the trigger that reopens the warehouse scan objective decision. | Capacity evidence remains nonrepresentative until the domain workload shape is approved. | Before benchmark execution |
| OD-023-087 | Manufacturing shift model | Performance Architecture | For Manufacturing shift model: domain-approved actors, active/concurrent distribution, transaction mix, payload/data shape, tenant/company/plant breadth, sustained/peak curve and replay script. The reviewer must be able to reproduce the manufacturing shift model measurement, trace the manufacturing shift model authority boundary and verify the manufacturing shift model recovery behavior. | Capacity evidence remains nonrepresentative until the domain workload shape is approved. | Before benchmark execution |
| OD-023-088 | Finance close model | Performance Architecture | For Finance close model: domain-approved actors, active/concurrent distribution, transaction mix, payload/data shape, tenant/company/plant breadth, sustained/peak curve and replay script. Close finance close model only with a versioned finance close model decision record, quantified pass/fail boundary and an owner for the residual finance close model risk. | Capacity evidence remains nonrepresentative until the domain workload shape is approved. | Before benchmark execution |
| OD-023-089 | Report cancellation method | Reporting | For Report cancellation method: workload class, query plan/rows, concurrency, refresh/schedule, timeout/cancel release, snapshot staleness, drill-down authority and OLTP test. The report cancellation method packet must compare options, name the selected report cancellation method limit and record a reversible report cancellation method acceptance test. | Reporting controls cannot be approved without certified semantics and measured transactional impact. | Before reporting workload approval |
| OD-023-090 | Dashboard refresh cadence | Reporting | For Dashboard refresh cadence: workload class, query plan/rows, concurrency, refresh/schedule, timeout/cancel release, snapshot staleness, drill-down authority and OLTP test. Evidence for dashboard refresh cadence also identifies the operating owner, rejected alternative and the trigger that reopens the dashboard refresh cadence decision. | Reporting controls cannot be approved without certified semantics and measured transactional impact. | Before reporting workload approval |

## Chapter 64 — Approval and Roadmap

Approval requires Architecture Board review plus performance, platform, database, operations, security, integration, reporting, domain, data-governance, audit and cost perspectives. Implementation remains unauthorized until workloads, datasets, telemetry, test environments, ownership and decision records are approved; FCSB-024 remains the next controlled volume.

### Governed performance contract

| Control lens | Named concern | Required outcome | Accountable authority |
|---|---|---|---|
| Requirement | review conditions | Define review conditions as a versioned contract with an explicit measurement point. | Performance Architecture |
| Evidence | implementation prerequisites | Supply scale-shaped evidence for implementation prerequisites without substituting an average or anecdote. | Domain owner |
| Protection | controlled next volume | Escalate controlled next volume when the approved workload exceeds safe capacity or authority is at risk. | Operations |

### Architecture views

**Governance and approval**

~~~mermaid
flowchart LR
    Governanceandapproval800["Evidence package"]
    Governanceandapproval801["Domain and technical review"]
    Governanceandapproval802["Architecture Board decision"]
    Governanceandapproval803["Implementation gate"]
    Governanceandapproval804["Roadmap handoff"]
    Governanceandapproval800 --> Governanceandapproval801
    Governanceandapproval801 --> Governanceandapproval802
    Governanceandapproval802 --> Governanceandapproval803
    Governanceandapproval803 --> Governanceandapproval804
~~~

### Approval conditions

Approval requires: an agreed critical-journey register; versioned workload and dataset schemas; authority-preserving budgets; tool and environment decisions; protected telemetry; load, stress, spike, soak, volume and recovery plans; tenant-fairness evidence; capacity and cost review; named operational ownership; and closure or accepted disposition of blocking open decisions.

### Version history

| Version | Date | Status | Change |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1.0 Draft | 2026-07-19 | Architecture Review Draft | Initial Performance and Scalability Architecture reference. |

### Roadmap handoff

Before FCSB-024 begins, reviewers should confirm that this volume establishes performance evidence requirements without selecting infrastructure or claiming an enterprise scale runtime. FCSB-024 remains **Product Governance and Release Architecture**; FCSB-025 remains **Product Roadmap and Future Vision**. This draft makes no application, schema, migration, seed, Docker, environment, API, frontend or test change and authorizes no implementation.
